Document :    nexus

Summary:
PLEASE READ THIS ENTIRE DESCRIPTION AND THE INCLUDED LINKS BEFORE TAKING ANY ACTION.

On July 7, 2024, security researchers disclosed the following vulnerability in the RADIUS protocol: 

CVE-2024-3596: RADIUS Protocol under RFC 2865 is susceptible to forgery attacks by an on-path attacker who can modify any valid Response (Access-Accept, Access-Reject, or Access-Challenge) to any other response using a chosen-prefix collision attack against MD5 Response Authenticator signature.

This bug has been filed against the product  to address the vulnerability known as BlastRADIUS and identified by CVE: CVE-2024-3596

PLEASE EVALUATE YOUR PRODUCT BY COMPLETING THE STEPS AS INDICATED AT:
https://ciscovulnmgmtprod.service-now.com/psirt?id=hotpage_preview&hot_page_id=cisco-sa-radius-spoofing-july-2024-87cCDwZ3#u_instructions_to_engineering

This tool will guide you in the evaluation of these vulnerabilities and will automate the documentation of this bug appropriately.

Additional information can be obtained in our hotpage at:
https://ciscovulnmgmtprod.service-now.com/psirt?id=hotpage_preview&hot_page_id=cisco-sa-radius-spoofing-july-2024-87cCDwZ3

VERY IMPORTANT NOTE ABOUT SEVERITY: Should the bug have a Severity of *issue_not_a_bug_or_enhancement (Sev7)* PLEASE RAISE THE SEVERITY TO 2.  The bug was raised initially as Sev 7 to overcome some CDETS limitations when filing hundreds of bugs for ALL potentially affected Cisco products.

Note: If you believe that this bug was opened against the wrong CDETS Component (or even Product) please update the bug as you see fit and our system will learn from your changes for the bug opening in future incidents.

Anil,
 
Just to be clear, as Terri said, we are not providing a "blanket exception" for the "Blast Radius" incident here... you will need to discuss fix plans for that with Mick/Ann/psirt-radius@cisco.com
 
What we are saying is that 8.4(10) cannot be moved from the current August release schedule plan, as it is needed for other incidents (NX-OS bundled publication, the recent command injection advisory).
So if the "Blast Radius fix" cannot fit that timeline due to its (understandable) complexity, you will have to agree on an alternative fix plan with the psirt-radius team - do a 8.4(10a), do an SMU (assuming this fix would be even SMUable) while waiting for 8.4(11), or just wait for 8.4(11)...

Marco

From: Anil Kunnath (anikunna) 
Sent: Monday, July 22, 2024 8:31 AM
To: Ann Chen (chenann) ; Terri Grant (tfinn) ; psirt-radius(mailer list) ; Marco Cassini (mcassini) 
Cc: Vasu Agrawal (vasagraw) ; Sarath Chandran Sasikumar (sarchand) ; Sheshadri Sreenath (shsreena) ; Sivaram Duraisamy (sivadura) ; Sankara Syamala (sasyamal) ; Sachin Joshi (sachinj) 
Subject: Re: CSCwk70839 - Blast RADIUS vulnerability
 
Hi PSIRT team,
For the Nexus N7K we have the following defect: CSCwk70838 to track the Blast Radius vulnerability.
The mitigation as per available documentation is to implement the Message-Authenticator attribute that uses HMAC-MD5 and make it a requirement in both request and response packets for Radius/UDP. This fix currently involves non-trivial code changes and test effort.
 
Since this has come late in the release cycle for 8.4.10, we are constrained to include this fix in the current release (8.4.10) planned for this month.
The plan is to have it implemented for 8.4.11 in January 2025 timeframe.
 
Please let us know if this is acceptable to target it for 8.4.11 and get an exception for 8.4.10.
 
Thanks,
Anil
---End of Note Titled: tbp-eval---

From: Marco Cassini 
Date: Wednesday, 14 May 2025 at 1:56?PM
To: Sankara Syamala (sasyamal) , Mukund Kamath (mukamath) , Sameer Kamalapurkar (samekama) , Anil Kunnath (anikunna) , Sugan Subramanian (sugan) , Mick Buchanan (mick)

Subject: Re: QR1F - PSIRTs & Double commits
+ Mick

Hi Sankara,

As far as I undestand, fixes for this are currently only available in 9.3.x and 10.2.x releases, based on the fact that later trains did support RadSec and DTLS, so those could be used as a "workaround" there. However, this implies the Radius server would need to support that too, that might not be available in all enviroments. And both those trains have reached end of software maintenance at this point, so not having the fix in a supported train is concerning.

I do think 10.6.1 should include a fix for this. It would look bad if we are releasing a new train almost a year after this vulnerability was announced, and we would still not incorporate a fix for this there. But I will let Mick comment, as he was the primary investigator for this issue.

Also, any particular reason why this is not being backported to at least one of the 10.4.x/10.5.x trains in an upcoming MR? Aside from what you do for 10.6.x, it will still be year+ before 10.6.x becomes the "main"/"recommended" train, so I do think having a fix in currently recommended train would make sense.

Thanks,

Marco

Hi Vasu,
 
Sorry for bothering you. Your reply would be very helpful.
 
Going through the bug enclosures(https://cdetsng.cisco.com/summary/#/defect/CSCwk70839) the following is my observation:
 
As per the IMPACT-ASSESSMENT:
A: "RADIUS for user authentication with TCP TLS ( From version 10.3.x) : Not impacted
RADIUS for user authentication with UDP DTLS (From version 10.4.x) : Not impacted

Subject: Re: CSCwk70839 - Blast RADIUS vulnerability
 
Hi PSIRT team,
For the Nexus N7K we have the following defect: CSCwk70838 to track the Blast Radius vulnerability.
The mitigation as per available documentation is to implement the Message-Authenticator attribute that uses HMAC-MD5 and make it a requirement in both request and response packets for Radius/UDP. This fix currently involves non-trivial code changes and test effort.
 
Since this has come late in the release cycle for 8.4.10, we are constrained to include this fix in the current release (8.4.10) planned for this month.
The plan is to have it implemented for 8.4.11 in January 2025 timeframe.
 
Please let us know if this is acceptable to target it for 8.4.11 and get an exception for 8.4.10.
 
Thanks,
Anil

From: Sarath Chandran Sasikumar (sarchand) 
Sent: Monday, July 15, 2024 12:45 PM
To: Ann Chen (chenann) 
Cc: Anil Kunnath (anikunna) ; Vasu Agrawal (vasagraw) ; Terri Grant (tfinn) 
Subject: CSCwk70839 - Blast RADIUS vulnerability

Hello Ann,

I just submitted the evaluation for CSCwk70839 (CVE-2024-3596). We went through the vendor guide mentioned in the psirt hotpage and also the researchers' blog.

We understand that there are two approaches to fix this
- A short term mitigation (\[^\"\])"+necessary but not sufficien(\[^\"\])"+) using the message authenticator attribute and
- A long term fix where TLS or DTLS is used as the secure transport.

 On the Nexus 9K, although our client implementation can parse and validate the incoming message authenticator attribute , it is not mandatory. It also doesn?t send it as part of the access request message. However, from version 10.3.1 onwards we support TLS mode (UDP DTLS from 10.4.x). They need to be explicitly enabled with configuration options .

1) Can we claim that Nexus 9000 running 10.3.1 and above are not vulnerable and ask customers to enable TLS/DTLS mode there ?
2) On older versions (9.3.x), we are vulnerable and are planning to address it by adding the ability to make the message authenticator mandatory - the short-term mitigation. This would be the plan for other Nexus platforms like the MDS and the 7000 series switches.
[ann] Could you let us know what the planned fix release number and what is your expected release date.


Let us know if this is okay. If you have details on how RADIUS clients on other Cisco platforms , that would be a great help. We would also like to know if you are aware if Cisco servers like ISE are planning to fix. It doesn?t seem like a mandatory attribute even there.

Thanks,
Sarath

---Start of Note Titled: Evaluation-PR2F (Created 07/23/2024 03:34:49 Added 07/23/2024 03:34:49 by qddts)---
10.5.X Release Train - RadSec (TLS) and DTLS support are available in the release. One of them can be used to address the vulnerability.

1) Long Term Solution - RadSec (TLS) or DTLS provided encapsulation is the actual solution to the problem.
2) Short Term Mitigation - The Message-Authenticator attribute contains the HMAC-MD5 hash (on the complete RADIUS payload) which is not prone to the attack exposed by the vulnerability. Including Message-Authenticator in the request-packets (e.g. access-request) sent to the server (e.g. ISE) and validating Message-Authenticator in the response-packets (e.g. Access-Accept, Access-Reject, or Access-Challenge) from the server can be used to mitigate the problem. 

Customers can use RadSec (TLS) or DTLS  which are present in the release as the best solution. As an alternative, we can plan to incorporate the Message-Authenticator support along with its CLI knob (to provide backward compatibility) in one of the upcoming maintenance/feature releases on this release-train.
---End of Note Titled: Evaluation-PR2F---


---Start of Note Titled:  Evaluation-Niles (Created 07/22/2024 23:46:45 Modified 07/23/2024 03:38:09 by vasagraw)---
10.3.X Release Train - RadSec (TLS) is available in the release, which can be used to address the vulnerability.

1) Long Term Solution - RadSec (TLS) or DTLS provided encapsulation is the actual solution to the problem.
2) Short Term Mitigation - The Message-Authenticator attribute contains the HMAC-MD5 hash (on the complete RADIUS payload) which is not prone to the attack exposed by the vulnerability. Including Message-Authenticator in the request-packets (e.g. access-request) sent to the server (e.g. ISE) and validating Message-Authenticator in the response-packets (e.g. Access-Accept, Access-Reject, or Access-Challenge) from the server can be used to mitigate the problem. 

Customers can use RadSec (TLS) which is present in the release as the best solution. As an alternative, we can plan to incorporate the Message-Authenticator support along with its CLI knob (to provide backward compatibility) in one of the upcoming maintenance/feature releases on this release-train.

---Start of Note Titled:  Release-note (Created 07/11/2024 15:36:59 Modified 05/04/2026 01:05:09 by vasagraw)---
<B>Symptom:</B>
This bug has been filed to evaluate the product  against the vulnerability in the RADIUS protocol disclosed on July 7, 2024 known as BlastRADIUS:

CVE-2024-3596 - RADIUS Protocol Spoofing Vulnerability (Blast-RADIUS)

This advisory is available at the following link:
https://sec.cloudapps.cisco.com/security/center/content/CiscoSecurityAdvisory/cisco-sa-radius-spoofing-july-2024-87cCDwZ3

<B>Conditions:</B>
When RADIUS on Nexus-9000 device is configured to operate without RadSec (TLS) or DTLS, it can be open to the potential attack.

Nexus-9000 device acts only as a RADIUS CLIENT.

If Nexus-9000 and the RADIUS server are in a protected network (e.g. VPN or private network), then also vulnerability would not be applicable.


This vulnerability impacts products only when configured to use the RADIUS protocol.
The product provides capabilities that will allow it to be put in a non vulnerable configuration. This section will be updated with information about those capabilities.

\\-->

<B>Workaround:</B>
Latest N9K release have got the RadSec (TLS) and DTLS Support. One of them can be configured to address the vulnerability.

10.3.X Release Train - RadSec (TLS) is present
10.4.X Release Train - Both RadSec (TLS) and DTLS are present
10.5.X Release Train - Both RadSec (TLS) and DTLS are present

Nexus-9000 devices acts only as a RADIUS CLIENT. Other RADIUS operations are not applicable. 

As a low level protection, customers can configure a lower value for 'timeout' config. It would increase the cost and efforts for a potential attack. 


Not available or not applicable.

<B>Further Problem Description:</B>
Additional details about the vulnerability listed above can be found at http://cve.mitre.org/cve/cve.html

*PSIRT Evaluation:*
The Cisco PSIRT has assigned this bug the following CVSS version 3.1 score. The Base CVSS scores as of the time of evaluation are 8.1:
https://tools.cisco.com/security/center/cvssCalculator.x?version=3.1&vector=CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:H/I:H/A:H

The Cisco PSIRT has assigned this score based on information obtained from multiple sources. This includes the
CVSS score assigned by the third-party vendor when available. The CVSS score assigned may not reflect the
actual impact on the Cisco Product.

Additional information on Cisco's security vulnerability policy can be found at the following URL:
https://tools.cisco.com/security/center/resources/security_vulnerability_policy.html


---End of Note Titled: Release-note---
